1904.] NEWCWANG.11 (Bceived 29th October 1904.) NATIVE OTUSTOM HOUSE, NEWOHWANG, 25TH OCrOBER 1904. SIR, I.-I have the honour to submit the statistics of the junk trade at this port for the third quarter of the year 1904. Dues and Duties on all goods arriving or leaving by sea-going junks were collected at Treaty Tariff rates, afd Likin at the rate of A per cent. ad valorem on Imports and 5 per cent. ad valorem on Exports exception was &#39;made, however, on certain specified articles, which were charged according to a reduced tariff. Junks engaging in up-river trade were required to pay Tonnage Dues, but no Duty was levied on their cargoes. The only changes in Revenue procedure during the quarter were the revision of the registration system and alterations in the Duties on Opium and Salt. Formerly all junks, regardless of their size or tonnage, paid registration fees of Hk.ft 2.50 annually; but the new system divides junks into 15 classes, according to their tonnage capacity, and these graduated fees are payable every six months. The Duty on Opium was Hk.a 40 per picul formerly, but it was raised to k.Th 6o per picul. At the same&#39;time the Duty on Salt, which was Rk.a i per tan of 6oo catties, was reduced to Hk.Ta o.6o. So far the result of these changes has been very satisfactory, both to the Customs and merchants. II. PART [V. NOTIFIOATIOtNS, Duing the Quarter ended September 80, 1904.
254 CUSTOMS 4GAZETTE. [J ULY-SEn., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 399. FOOCHOW DISTRICT. TUNGYUNG LIGHTHOUSE. NOTICE is hereby given tha the light on Tungyung was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the Ist July 1904. The illuminating apparatus is group flaphing of the first order, showing three white flashes in quick succession every 20 seconds. The light-tower is situated on the eastern slope of the island, and the light, which is elevated 325 feet -bove the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 25 nautical miles in all directions, except where it is obscured by the island from south rotind by east to N. 7o E. Bearings are magnetic and from seaward. The tower is round, of brick, 24 feet high, with a total height from base to lantern vane of 461 feet. The tower and dwellings are painted white. Approximate position Latitude . . . . . . . . . . ..* . 260 22&#39; 4o&quot; N. Longitude . . . . . . . . . . . 120 3o 25&quot; E. FoG GuN SIGNAL. The Keepers at this station on hearing a bell, fog horn, steam whistle, or any other sound, during foggy or thick weather, indicating the proximity of a vessel, will fire 2 guns with an interval of I minute between them, and if the vessel&#39;s fog signal-showing that she is under way-continues to be heard, will repeat the firing after an interval of 15 minutes. SHANGHAI, 4th ulty 1904.
1904.] NOTmIATIOs&amp;amp; No. 400. AMOY DISTRICT. SHOAL PATCH IN APPROACH TO AMOY HARBOUR. NoTiCE is hereby given that the Commanding Officer (Commander C. E. MoNRO, R.N.) of H.B.M.S. Rambler reports the existence of a shoal patch in the approach to Amoy bour, with Chapel Island bearing S. 8* E., distant 6.1 miles, and Chin-ha Point (S.E. point) bearing N. 870 W., distant 4.6 miles. The least water at present obtained is 31 fathoms at low water of spring tides. A further examination will be made. Also, a shoal of small extent, with Quemoy Pagoda bearing N. 170 E., distant 6-IT miles, and Taitan Lighthouse bearing N. 470 W., with 41 fathoms at low water of spring tides. All bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 218t JUly 1904. No. 401. NEWCHWANG DISTRICT. THE following Notifications, issued by the Harbour Master at Newchwang, are hereby published for general information &quot;LOCAL NOTICE TO MARINERS SPECIAL NO. I1. &quot;Dawger8 to Navigation: Sunlen Vesel. &quot;Notice is hereby given that the Japanese steamer Yatsuna Maru lies sunk in the Newchwang harbour off the Custom House in mid-stream in 6 fathoms at low water. &quot;She lies with her bow up-stream, showing about 12 feet of her masts above water, and, until removed, a sampan will be made fast to the wreck, showing a red flag during the day and a red light 4uring the night. &quot;. APPENDIX . KOWLOON, LAPPA, AND KIAOCHOW: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JULY-SEPTEMBER 1904.
278 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JULY-SEPT., (Received 17th October 1904.) OUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, oan OCTOBER 1904. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1904. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan (; (k), Lintin (fo f), and Shamchun (g 4), on the north and west; Samun (g ) on the east; and Shafichung ( A M ) and Shatowkok (W A ), on the Mire Bay littoral. The Custom House is called IL a a |. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.-a. (Received 24th October 1904.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KIAOCHOW, 19Tx OCTOBER 1904. SIR, L-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this, prt for the third quarter of the year 1904. b. The Custom House (W W 3) was opened on the ist July 1899. The collection of Tariff Import and xport Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Ltk.Th 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties and, Lkin on Junk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day. The stations at present provisionally controlled by the Kiaochow Customs are known as Tsangkou (1 13); Nuku ( W); Shatskou (V T 13), including Tengyao (N Z); Hungshihyai (f ? M); Taputou ( X $ ); and Lingshanwei (3 1 $), including Tachiangkou (ff WP). c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from Chins, the, statistics of goods landed and consumed in German territory not being included.
